Let me analyze the loop and the RPs to understand the data structure traversal.

Looking at the loop body:
```c
while (v) {
    t = v -> next;
    v-> next = w;
    w-> prev = v; 
    w = v;
    v = t;
    if (v) {
        v -> prev = 0;
        t = v -> next;
        v-> next = w;
        w-> prev = v; 
        w = v;
        v = t;
        if (v) {
            v -> prev = 0;
        }
    }
}
```

This is a loop that processes the list by pairs of nodes. It takes two nodes at a time, reverses their links, and attaches them to `w`. `w` starts as `q`, and `v` starts as `p`.

Let me understand each iteration:

Iteration 1 (RP_pre_loop → RP_iter_2):
- Starting state at RP_pre_loop: v = p@pre, w = q@pre
  - We have `dlistrep_shape(p@pre, 0) * dlistrep_shape(q@pre, 0)`
  - Stack: v = p@pre, w = q@pre, t = undef

After the first loop body (processes two nodes from p):
The loop body does:
1. t = v->next  (t = p@pre->next, which is the second node of p's list)
2. v->next = w  (p@pre->next = q@pre)
3. w->prev = v  (q@pre->prev = p@pre)
4. w = v  (w = p@pre)
5. v = t  (v = the second node)
6. if (v) { v->prev = 0; ... } -- second node exists, so:
   t = v->next (advances to third node)
   v->next = w (second node's next = p@pre)
   w->prev = v (p@pre->prev = second node)
   w = v (w = second node)
   v = t (v = third node)
   if (v) { v->prev = 0; } -- third node exists, so v->prev = 0

Let me think about what the invariant should look like:

The loop is reversing the list `p` node by node (in pairs) and attaching each reversed pair to `w` (which starts as `q`). After processing:
- `w` points to the head of the reversed portion (the most recently processed node)
- `v` points to the remaining unreversed portion of the original `p` list

- `w` is the head of the already-reversed portion

1. The reversed portion from `w` back to `q@pre` (going "backwards" via prev links, but actually this is a DLL and the nodes have been reversed)

Actually no, the dllseg definition says: first param x is segment start, last param y is endpoint (NOT in segment). So dllseg_shape(A, 0, p@pre, q@pre) means segment from A to q@pre, with A as start, p@pre as last node inside segment. The segment contains nodes A, ..., p@pre. Since A->next = p@pre and p@pre->next = q@pre, the segment has A (->next = p@pre) and p@pre (->next = q@pre, which is the endpoint). Wait, that's not right either.

Let me re-read dllseg definition:
```
dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || 
  exists z, data_at(field_addr(x, next), struct list*, z) * 
            data_at(field_addr(x, prev), struct list*, xp) * 
            dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)
```

So dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y):
- Base case: x == y (segment is empty). In this case xp == yp is also required.
- Recursive case: x->next = z, x->prev = xp, then dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)

This means:
- x: start of segment
- xp: x's prev pointer value
- yp: last node INSIDE the segment (yp->next == y)
- y: first node OUTSIDE/after the segment

OK so in RP_iter_2 with w=A: dllseg_shape(A, 0, p@pre, q@pre):
- A->prev = 0 ✓
- A->next must lead eventually to p@pre which has next = q@pre
- A->next = p@pre, p@pre->next = q@pre
- So: dllseg_shape(A, 0, p@pre, q@pre) = 
  data_at(A->next, p@pre) * data_at(A->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(p@pre, A, p@pre, q@pre)
  
  dllseg_shape(p@pre, A, p@pre, q@pre) = 
  data_at(p@pre->next, q@pre) * data_at(p@pre->prev, A) * dllseg_shape(q@pre, p@pre, p@pre, q@pre)
  
  dllseg_shape(q@pre, p@pre, p@pre, q@pre):
  Is q@pre == q@pre? Yes. And p@pre == p@pre? Yes. So emp. ✓

- v appears as arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(v, 0). ✓
- t is assigned in loop body, but after `t = v->next`, t is used. t's value comes from v->next, which is covered by dlistrep_shape(v, 0). The permission to READ v->next comes from dlistrep_shape(v, 0) (which unfolds to expose data_at for v->next and v->prev). t doesn't need its own has_permission because QCP creates a local variable for it.
- p and q: are they used in the loop body? Looking at the loop: p and q are not directly accessed in the loop body (they're function parameters). They're read-only. Actually, they appear in the LOCAL section and have data_at bindings:
  - data_at(p_283_addr, struct list*, p@pre)
  - data_at(q_280_addr, struct list*, q@pre)
  
  But these are LOCAL bindings, not part of the invariant. The invariant replaces the state, but p and q are only used in parameters/Require, not in the loop body.
  
Actually, wait, I need to check the post-loop code too. After the while loop, `return w;`. So only w is used post-loop. v isn't used post-loop. But the Ensure says `dlistrep_shape(__return, 0)`, which means the return value (w) must be a complete DLL.

When the loop exits (v == 0), the invariant gives:
dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(0, 0) = dlistrep_shape(w, 0)
Which matches the Ensure dlistrep_shape(__return, 0). ✓

But wait, does the loop body actually access p@pre or q@pre? Looking at the loop:
- t = v->next: t gets v->next
- v->next = w: writes to v->next
- w->prev = v: writes to w->prev -- this accesses w, which is in dlistrep_shape(w, 0)
- w = v: reassigns w
- v = t: reassigns v

In the "if (v)" block:
- v->prev = 0: writes to v->prev (covered by dlistrep_shape(v, 0))
- t = v->next: reads v->next
- v->next = w: writes to v->next
- w->prev = v: writes to w->prev -- but w and v could be the same node? No, v was advanced past the node that became w. But w->prev = v accesses the current w node's prev field.

Actually, w->prev = v in the inner block: w is the previous node (p@pre or the head of the reversed chain), and v is the current node being processed. w->prev should be accessible since w is in dlistrep_shape(w, 0).

But wait: dlistrep_shape(w, 0) gives ownership of w's prev and next fields. ✓
dlistrep_shape(v, 0) gives ownership of v's prev and next fields. ✓

The issue is that when we do `w = v` and `v = t`, the predicates' first arguments change. The invariant dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) needs to be preserved through these assignments.

After processing a node:
- We've modified v->next and w->prev, effectively moving a node from v's list to w's list
- Then w = v (w now points to the transferred node)
- v = t (v now points to the next unprocessed node)

The invariant should hold: dlistrep_shape(new_w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(new_v, 0). Does the loop body preserve this?

Let me verify with the actual operations on the first node:
1. t = v->next: t gets the second node. v->next is accessible via dlistrep_shape(v, 0).
2. v->next = w: sets the first node's next to w. This transfers the first node from v's list to w's list. Now v's first node points to w, so the dlistrep_shape starting at v includes w's entire list.
3. w->prev = v: sets w's prev to v. This is OK because w is in dlistrep_shape(w, 0) which gives ownership of w->prev.
4. w = v: w now points to v's first node.
5. v = t: v now points to the second node.

After these 5 steps:
- dlistrep_shape(w, 0): w = first node. first_node->next = old_w (from step 2). old_w->prev = first_node (from step 3). So w's chain is: first_node → old_w → ... → NULL. ✓
- dlistrep_shape(v, 0): v = second node (original v->next). This is the original sublist of v without the first node. ✓

Then the inner block processes the second node similarly, attaching it to w's list. After which:
- w = second node
- v = third node

And the invariant still holds. The invariant `dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0)` is indeed preserved!

What about PROP constraints?

Looking at the RPs:

- RP_iter_2 PROP: (p@pre != 0), (q@pre != 0), (y_303 != 0), (y_311 != 0)
- RP_iter_3 PROP: (y_311 != 0), (p@pre != 0), (q@pre != 0), (y_303 != 0), (y_311 != 0), (y_315 != 0), (y_327 != 0)

The PROP items have both p@pre != 0 and q@pre != 0, plus various exists variables != 0.

The invariant should include:
- q@pre != 0: This is from the guard `if (w)` at line 17, where w = q. Since we only enter the while loop if w (which is q) != 0, and q is never modified, q != 0 always. Wait, but in the code q is never modified in the loop body. So q != 0 is always true.

- What about p@pre? In RP_pre_loop PROP, p@pre != 0 is NOT present. That suggests p could be NULL in pre_loop. But in iter_2 it shows p@pre != 0. This is because the loop body only executes when v != 0, and v starts as p. If p were NULL, the while loop would not execute, and we'd never reach iter_2.

But the invariant must hold in ALL iterations (pre_loop, iter_2, iter_3, ...). If p could be NULL in pre_loop, the invariant can't include p@pre != 0.

- v != 0: In pre_loop, v = p. p could be NULL or non-NULL. The while loop only runs when v != 0. But the invariant placement (`/* INFILL */`) is at line 18, right before the `while (v)` at line 19.

Actually wait, looking at the code structure:
```c
    if (w) {
      /* INFILL */
      while (v) {
```

The `/* INFILL */` is the loop invariant. But looking at the unrolled version, the invariant check point would be at the loop head, where v is tested.

For the invariant to hold at loop entry: need to handle case where v might be 0 or non-0.

In RP_pre_loop: v = p@pre, PROP has only (q@pre != 0). If p@pre could be 0, then v could be 0, and the loop wouldn't execute. But the invariant should still hold.

Actually, I need to think about what PROP constraints are needed:
1. From the loop guard analysis: the loop is only entered when `v != 0`. The invariant is at the loop head, so at entry: v = p. We enter the loop body only when v != 0. So v might be 0 or not when first checking.
2. w != 0: w = q at invariant point. We know q != 0 from the `if (w)` guard. So w != 0 at invariant point? It should be since we only get here if w != 0. But what about successive iterations? w gets reassigned and could theoretically become 0... but no, w always gets assigned to a node that was v (which was != 0 since we're in the loop). So w != 0 always.

Wait, what if v starts as 0? Then `while (v)` is false immediately. The invariant at the loop head: v = 0, w = q (q != 0). dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(0, 0) = dlistrep_shape(q, 0) * emp = dlistrep_shape(q, 0). This should match the pre-loop state (minus p's list, since p=0 and dlistrep_shape(0, 0) = emp).

In the pre-loop state (outside while), we have dlistrep_shape(p,0) * dlistrep_shape(q,0). With p=0, this is emp * dlistrep_shape(q,0) = dlistrep_shape(q,0). ✓

Without any PROP constraints! But wait, let me check the RP_iter_2 PROP: p@pre != 0, q@pre != 0. If these are essential for the invariant preservation, I might need them.

Actually, PROP constraints like p@pre != 0 are about the initial values of function parameters. The invariant doesn't need to restate them. The proof system knows that function parameters are non-null from the preconditions.

Hmm, but for the invariant to be self-contained, should I include any PROP? Let me think about what's strictly needed.

For `dlistrep_shape(v, 0)`: Same. If v could be 0, it's emp.

So the invariant works without any PROP constraints. The SEP * operator handles empty lists naturally.

Looking at the SKILL.md: "Every element of the invariant (SEP predicates, PROP constraints) must be consistent with ALL RPs." But p and q don't appear in any SEP predicate of the invariant. They only appear in LOCAL (which is auto-managed). So they shouldn't need to be in the invariant.

dllseg_shape(z, w, w, w->next):
We need this segment from z to w->next, with last node = w. That means z->next... eventually reaches w, and w->next = w->next.

But this is circular (z → ... → w → w->next). Since z = w->next (from the first expansion), we have w->next → ... → w. This is a cycle, which shouldn't happen in a well-formed DLL.

So dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w->next) doesn't work as a single-node segment. The base case requires yp == y but also x == y for empty segment. For a non-empty segment with one node, x != y, and the segment covers x with x->next as the next node.

OK I think this approach is too convoluted. Let me think of a completely different invariant structure.

What if I use:
```
dlistrep_shape(w, v) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)
```

Wait, this overlaps even worse (v appears in both predicates' chains).

Let me step way back. The loop body is:
```
t = v->next;
v->next = w;
w->prev = v;
w = v;
v = t;
...
```

The first three operations are moving node v from v's list to w's list. Let me think about what QCP does step by step:

1. **t = v->next**: Unfold dlistrep_shape(v, 0) to get v->next. State:
   - data_at(v->next, old_v_next)  
   - data_at(v->prev, 0)
   - dlistrep_shape(old_v_next, v)
   - dlistrep_shape(w, 0)
   - data_at(t, old_v_next) [assign result]

2. **v->next = w**: Write w to the already-exposed v->next. State:
   - data_at(v->next, w)
   - data_at(v->prev, 0)
   - dlistrep_shape(old_v_next, v)
   - dlistrep_shape(w, 0)
   - data_at(t, old_v_next)

3. **w->prev = v**: Need to write to w->prev. State:
   - dlistrep_shape(w, 0) is folded, w->prev not exposed
   - QCP tries to create undef_data_at for w->prev from dlistrep_shape(w, 0)
   - Only builtin strategies, no dll_nodata
   - FAILS

The fix would be if w->prev was somehow already exposed. But there's no read of w->prev to trigger the expansion.

What if the invariant explicitly had `data_at(field_addr(w, prev), ...)` as part of a predicate? But the invariant format only uses shape predicates...

Unless I can write the invariant differently to trigger the expansion. What if I add a "dummy" condition that forces w's node to be expanded?

Actually, wait. Let me look at the strategies file to see what builtin rule 14 does.

Hmm, I can't read the strategies file (it's not in the whitelist). But the SKILL says `builtin/common.strategies` is used.

Let me try another approach. What if I separate w's node from the rest of its list explicitly using dllseg?

```
/*@ Inv Assert 
    dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w->next) *
    dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) *
    dlistrep_shape(v, 0) *
    has_permission(&t)
 */
```

Actually wait, this still has the field expression `w->next` in multiple places, which could cause dup_data_at.

Hmm, what about:
```
/*@ Inv Assert 
    exists w_next,
    dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next) *
    dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) *
    dlistrep_shape(v, 0) *
    has_permission(&t)
 */
```

dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next):
- x = w, xp = 0, yp = w, y = w_next
- Recursive: data_at(w->next, z) * data_at(w->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z, w, w, w_next)

But dllseg_shape(z, w, w, w_next): we need the segment from z to w_next with last node = w. This means z must be w_next for the segment to be empty... but that doesn't make sense. 

Actually, for a segment with exactly ONE node (w), where w is both the start AND the last node:
dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next):
- w != w_next (in general)
- data_at(w->next, z) * data_at(w->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z, w, w, w_next)
- dllseg_shape(z, w, w, w_next): base case requires z == w_next AND w == w. So z must be w_next.
- But then data_at(w->next, w_next) — which is already covered by dlistrep_shape(w_next, w)!
- dlistrep_shape(w_next, w): data_at(w_next->next, ...) * data_at(w_next->prev, w) * ...

So there's a dup_data_at on w->next! dllseg_shape produces data_at(w->next, w_next), and dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) produces data_at(w_next->prev, w). These are different fields, so no duplication on the SAME field. w->next and w_next->prev are different locations.

Wait: `data_at(w->next, w_next)` and `data_at(w_next->prev, w)`. These are:
- w->next (field at &(w.next)) = w_next
- w_next->prev (field at &(w_next.prev)) = w

These are different memory locations! No dup_data_at!

So the invariant:
```
exists w_next,
dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next) *
dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) *
dlistrep_shape(v, 0) *
has_permission(&t)
```

This should work! Let me verify:

dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next):
data_at(w->next, w_next) * data_at(w->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(w_next, w, w, w_next)

dllseg_shape(w_next, w, w, w_next):
x = w_next, y = w_next. x == y ✓. But xp = w, yp = w. xp == yp ✓. So base case: emp.

Total expansion: data_at(w->next, w_next) * data_at(w->prev, 0) * emp

So dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next) = data_at(w->next, w_next) * data_at(w->prev, 0)

And dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) = (w_next == 0 && emp) || exists z, data_at(w_next->next, z) * data_at(w_next->prev, w) * dlistrep_shape(z, w_next)

The full invariant is:
data_at(w->next, w_next) * data_at(w->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)

This explicitly has data_at(w->prev, 0) which is EXACTLY what QCP needs to write to w->prev!

Now let me verify against the RPs:

data_at(q@pre->next, w_next) * data_at(q@pre->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, q@pre) * dlistrep_shape(p@pre, 0) * has_permission(&t)
= dlistrep_shape(q@pre, 0) * dlistrep_shape(p@pre, 0) * has_permission(&t)

Matches! (w_next is q@pre->next) ✓

A->next = p@pre, A->prev = 0.
= data_at(A->next, p@pre) * data_at(A->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(p@pre, A) * dlistrep_shape(B, 0)

dlistrep_shape(p@pre, A):
data_at(p@pre->next, q@pre) * data_at(p@pre->prev, A) * dlistrep_shape(q@pre, p@pre)

dlistrep_shape(q@pre, p@pre):
data_at(q@pre->next, y_307) * data_at(q@pre->prev, p@pre) * dlistrep_shape(y_307, q@pre)

Matches the SEP in RP_iter_2! ✓

data_at(C->next, w_next) * data_at(C->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, C) * dlistrep_shape(D, 0) * has_permission(&t)
C->next = y_311 (B)
= data_at(C->next, B) * data_at(C->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(B, C) * dlistrep_shape(D, 0)

dlistrep_shape(B, C):
data_at(B->next, y_303) * data_at(B->prev, C) * dlistrep_shape(y_303, B)

dlistrep_shape(y_303, B): ... (expands to A, then p@pre, then q@pre...)

Matches RP_iter_3! ✓

So the invariant with `exists w_next, dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)` should work!

Wait, I just realized: `dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next)` — the parameter `w` appears as both `x` (arg[0]) and `yp` (arg[2]). Given the definition:

dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y):
- x: segment start
- xp: start node's prev
- yp: last node inside segment
- y: endpoint marker (yp->next == y)

So dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next):
- x = w (start), xp = 0 (w->prev = 0)
- yp = w (last node), y = w_next (w->next = w_next)

Since x == yp == w, this is a 1-node segment. The segment is just w.

If z = w_next: base case gives emp.

- x = w_304_value, y = w_next_305
- If w_304_value == w_next_305: needs also 0 == w_304_value for base case. 
  If w_304_value != 0: base case fails, need recursive.
  If w_304_value == 0: base case succeeds (0 == w_next_305 && 0 == 0).
- The recursive case: data_at(w_304_value->next, z) * data_at(w_304_value->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(z, w_304_value, w_304_value, w_next_305)

  dllseg_shape(z, w_304_value, w_304_value, w_next_305):
  Base: z == w_next_305 && w_304_value == w_304_value ✓ (if z == w_next_305)
  So z must equal w_next_305 for the base case to fire.

  This gives: data_at(w_304_value->next, w_next_305) * data_at(w_304_value->prev, 0) * emp

So the expansion is correct. But QCP's assignment handler can't do this expansion.

The problem is that the write handler tries to match:
```
dllseg_shape(w_304_value, 0, w_304_value, w_next_305) * dlistrep_shape(w_next_305, w_304_value) * ...
|-- undef_data_at(&(w_304_value->prev), ...)
```

And only builtin rule 14 is applied, which doesn't know about dllseg_shape.

I think this is a fundamental limitation of QCP's assignment handling. But maybe there's a workaround I haven't thought of yet.

The assignment handler needs to find w->prev in the LHS. To do this, it needs to expand dllseg_shape(w, 0, w, w_next) to get data_at(w->prev, 0), then convert to undef_data_at(w->prev, ...).

The builtin strategies handle the data_at → undef_data_at conversion, but they don't handle dllseg_shape → data_at expansion. That requires dll_nodata strategies.

I think this is a bug or limitation of QCP. Read operations use custom strategies (dll_nodata) but write operations only use builtin strategies.

Wait, but w->next is accessed VIA the list traversal. When we do `t = v->next`, we access v->next which IS covered by dlistrep_shape(v, 0). And v IS at position arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(v, 0). So v->next is accessible.

For w, w->prev is accessed by `w->prev = v`. With PROP creating data_at(w->prev, 0), this should be writable.

But what about w->next? Is it ever accessed? Looking at the loop body again:

```
t = v -> next;       // reads v->next, not w->next
v-> next = w;        // writes to v->next, not w->next
w-> prev = v;        // writes to w->prev
w = v;               // stack assignment
v = t;               // stack assignment
if (v) {
    v -> prev = 0;   // writes to v->prev
    t = v -> next;   // reads v->next
    v-> next = w;    // writes to v->next
    w-> prev = v;    // writes to w->prev (now different w)
    w = v;           // stack assignment
    v = t;           // stack assignment
    if (v) {
        v -> prev = 0;  // writes to v->prev
    }
}
```

w->next is NEVER accessed! Only w->prev is written.

But wait, the `w = v` reassignment changes w. After this, the OLD w (which was w_299_value) doesn't correspond to the variable w anymore. The new w points to the node that was previously v. This means w->prev for the NEW w refers to the new node's prev field, which IS covered by dlistrep_shape(new_w, ?).

Hmm, but after `w = v`, the NEW w is the node that was just processed (previously v). Its prev was already set to 0 (from the pre-state of v). Now we don't access new-w->prev in the same iteration (we do in the inner block, where it's written again).

This is getting complicated. Let me just try the invariant and see what happens.

Actually, I also just realized: replacing the invariant is DESTRUCTIVE. The invariant replaces the entire program state. If I use PROP `w->prev == 0`, QCP will try to verify this constraint at the loop head and then use it to create the corresponding data_at.

But will QCP actually create data_at for w->prev from the PROP? Or will it just check the constraint and not create data_at?

From SKILL.md's dup_data_at section, it seems that PROP field expressions DO create data_at. So `w->prev == 0` should create data_at(w->prev, 0) in the state.

Let me try it. But also, I need to make sure the format is correct. Let me try the inline format:

```
/*@ Inv Assert w->prev == 0 && dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t) */
```

Hmm actually, I need to be careful about `&&` vs `*`. In QCP, `&&` joins PROP constraints, and `*` joins SEP predicates. But the whole invariant might need them to be separated differently.

Looking at the SKILL.md example:
```
/*@ Inv Assert exists p_prev,
    dllseg_shape(l, 0, p_prev, p) *
    dlistrep_shape(p, p_prev)
 */
```

No PROP, no `&&`. The invariant is purely SEP predicates with `*` joining.

What if I try:
```
/*@ Inv Assert w->prev == 0 &&
    dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) *
    dlistrep_shape(v, 0) *
    has_permission(&t)
 */
```

Or maybe QCP uses `*` for both PROP and SEP? Like:
```
w->prev == 0 * dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * ...
```

I'm not sure. Let me just try and see. Actually, let me look at the QCP log to see how it parsed the invariant when I used dllseg_shape. In the log:

```
Get Invariant: 
-------Assertion begin---------
exists w_next_298 w_299_value v_300_value,
( &t == t_294_addr ) && 
( &v == v_297_addr ) && 
( &w == w_291_addr ) && 
data_at(w_291_addr, ...) * 
dllseg_shape(...) * 
dlistrep_shape(...) * 
...
```

In the parsed output, LOCAL constraints use `&&` and SEP predicates use `*`. The LOCAL constraints are auto-generated for variables that appear in the invariant.

But I didn't write any LOCAL constraints in my invariant. QCP auto-generates them.

The question is: does QCP's parser for the invariant annotation accept `&&` for PROP constraints mixed with `*` for SEP predicates?

I'll try a few formats and see which one works. Actually, let me try the simplest approach first: just use `has_permission` for w->prev.

Wait, SKILL.md says has_permission is for stack variables, not heap fields. So `has_permission(&(w->prev))` won't work for a heap field.

Let me try the PROP approach with the mixed format. I'll write:

```
/*@ Inv Assert w->prev == 0 && dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t) */
```

And hope QCP handles the mixed `&&`/`*` correctly.

Actually, thinking more about this... the SKILL.md says:
"Overall form: PROP1 && PROP2 && ... && SEP1 * SEP2 * ..."

This implies PROP items (joined by `&&`) come before SEP items (joined by `*`), with all of them in a single assertion string. The `&&` and `*` are at the same level. Something like:

```
w->prev == 0 && dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)
```

But this syntax is unusual because `&&` has lower precedence than `*`. In C, `a && b * c` is `a && (b * c)`, not `(a && b) * c`. But in QCP's assertion parser, maybe both operators are separators at the same level.

Actually, looking at the QCP log parsing:
```
( &t == t_294_addr ) && ( &v == v_297_addr ) && ( &w == w_291_addr ) && data_at(...) * dllseg_shape(...) * ...
```

The `&&` separates LOCAL and the `*` separates SEP. But they're all at the same level of the assertion. The parser seems to handle `&&` and `*` as equivalent separators.

So: `w->prev == 0 && dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)` should parse as:
- PROP: w->prev == 0
- SEP: dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)

Let me try this!

But wait, there might be an issue. The PROP constraint `w->prev == 0` references `w` (a program variable) and `w->prev` (a field expression). For this to work, `w` must already be in the invariant's stack bindings. And it is, since `dlistrep_shape(w->next, w)` references `w`. So QCP should auto-generate `data_at(w_291_addr, struct list*, w_value)` for w's stack cell, and then create data_at(w_value->prev, 0) for the field.

Let me also consider: does `w->prev == 0` need to hold at all loop heads? Let me check:
- RP_pre_loop: w = q@pre, q@pre->prev = 0 ✓
- RP_iter_2: w = y_303, y_303->prev = 0 ✓
- RP_iter_3: w = y_315, y_315->prev = 0 ✓

Yes! w->prev == 0 at all loop heads.

Let me try this invariant and run the verification.

[TEXT]
I see -- the write handler for `w->prev = v` uses only builtin strategies, not `dll_nodata` strategies that know how to expand `dlistrep_shape`/`dllseg_shape`. The fix: use a PROP constraint with a field expression to force QCP to create an explicit `data_at` for `w->prev`, while the SEP part uses `dlistrep_shape(w->next, w)` (which does NOT cover `w->prev`).

```
RHS: exists w_next w_prev, w_prev == 0, data_at(q@pre->prev, w_prev), data_at(q@pre->next, w_next), dlistrep_shape(w_next, q@pre), dlistrep_shape(p@pre, 0)
```

Wait, but on the LHS, w = q@pre. The invariant should have `data_at(w->prev, 0)` and `dlistrep_shape(w->next, w)`, which with w=q@pre becomes `data_at(q@pre->prev, 0)` and `dlistrep_shape(q@pre->next, q@pre)`.

But the QCP goal has:
RHS: `data_at(&((q_pre) # "list" ->s "prev"), w_prev) * data_at(&((q_pre) # "list" ->s "next"), w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, q_pre) * dlistrep_shape(p_pre, 0)` with `w_prev == 0`.

So QCP expanded `w->prev == 0` into the PROP constraint and data_at for w->prev and w->next. But the LHS has `dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0)` which IS:
data_at(q_pre->next, ...) * data_at(q_pre->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(..., q_pre)

So the LHS and RHS are essentially the same modulo the data_at stack bindings. The entailment should be:
dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0) * dlistrep_shape(p_pre, 0) |- exists w_next, data_at(q_pre->prev, 0) * data_at(q_pre->next, w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, q_pre) * dlistrep_shape(p_pre, 0)

This is basically expanding dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0) = data_at(q_pre->next, w_next) * data_at(q_pre->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, q_pre), which should be provable by dll_nodata strategies.

But the strategies can't prove it! Why? Let me look at the LHS more carefully. The LHS has:
- PROP: q_pre != 0
- SEP: stack bindings + dlistrep_shape(p_pre, 0) + dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0)

The RHS has:
- PROP: w_prev = 0
- SEP: data_at(q_pre->prev, w_prev) + data_at(q_pre->next, w_next) + dlistrep_shape(w_next, q_pre) + dlistrep_shape(p_pre, 0)

The difference is that LHS has dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0) and RHS has deconstructed q_pre's node with explicit data_at.

dlistrep_shape(q_pre, 0) should expand to:
exists z, data_at(q_pre->next, z) * data_at(q_pre->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, q_pre)

Since q_pre != 0 (from PROP), the non-null branch of dlistrep_shape applies.

This is saying: after processing, the state should have:

- v's old next was 0 (end of list): dlistrep_shape(0, 0) = emp

LHS has v->next=w, v->prev=0. So v is at the head position. The RHS wants to fold v back into a dlistrep_shape.

Wait, dlistrep_shape(0, v): base case (0==0 && emp)... actually the definition is:
dlistrep_shape(l, p) = (l == 0 && emp) || exists t, data_at(field_addr(l, next), t) * data_at(field_addr(l, prev), p) * dlistrep_shape(t, l)

So dlistrep_shape(0, v) = (0 == 0 && emp) = emp. OK.

RHS: data_at(v->prev, 0) * data_at(v->next, w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, v) * dlistrep_shape(0, 0)

So we need to fold:
data_at(v->next, w) * data_at(v->prev, 0) * data_at(w->prev, v) * data_at(w->next, w_next_2) * dlistrep_shape(w_next_2, w)

Into:
data_at(v->next, w_next) * data_at(v->prev, 0) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, v)

dlistrep_shape(w, v) = data_at(w->next, w_next_2) * data_at(w->prev, v) * dlistrep_shape(w_next_2, w)

= data_at(v->next, w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, v)... no, that should be data_at(v->next, w) * dlistrep_shape(w, v)

Which is dlistrep_shape(v, 0) = data_at(v->prev, 0) * data_at(v->next, w_next) * dlistrep_shape(w_next, v).

So the RHS is dlistrep_shape(v, 0) (plus dlistrep_shape(0,0)=emp, plus t's binding).

The part in parentheses is: dlistrep_shape(w, v) [expanded].

= dlistrep_shape(v, 0) (with v->next = w, v->prev = 0, and dlistrep_shape(w, v) as the continuation)

Which equals the RHS! So the entailment should be provable. But the strategies can't prove it.

It seems like the strategies can't handle the forward/backward folding of dlistrep_shape in the presence of data_at. The issue is that the strategies are trying to match the LHS against the RHS, but the LHS has expanded data_at nodes while the RHS has folded dlistrep_shape.

- data_at(w->prev, 0) is already there as an explicit data_at
- Write v to it: data_at(w->prev, v)

- Old_v->next = old_w (from v->next = w), so data_at(new_w->next, old_w) should match
- dlistrep_shape(old_w, new_w) is the rest

But the invariant REPLACES the state. So after the invariant is established, the state IS dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0), which is folded.

For the loop body execution, QCP starts from this folded state. The first operation is `t = v->next`, which triggers expansion of dlistrep_shape(v, 0). v's fields are exposed. Then `v->next = w` modifies the exposed data_at. Then `w->prev = v` tries to write to w->prev, which is still folded in dlistrep_shape(w, 0).

So the issue is clear: w's list is never expanded because there's no read of w's fields.

What if the code had a read of w->next or w->prev before the write? Like `t = w->prev; w->prev = v;`? Then w's predicate would be expanded. But the code is fixed.

[TEXT]
Here is the summary of the loop invariant inference for `rev_append_twice.c`:

**Result: QCP_VERIFIED: NO**

**What was done:**

1. **Step 1 - Symbolic execution and state collection**: Unrolled the `while` loop (depth 3), ran `symexec` on the unrolled code, and parsed 3 recording points (RP_pre_loop, RP_iter_2, RP_iter_3). All gates passed.

2. **Step 2 - Invariant inference**: Analyzed the pairwise reversal loop. The loop processes `v` (head of remaining p-list) in pairs, reversing each and attaching to `w` (head of merged reversed+q list). The invariant was inferred: `dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)` -- two separate DLLs that partition the heap.

3. **Step 3 - Verification**: The original invariant (`dlistrep_shape(w, 0) * dlistrep_shape(v, 0) * has_permission(&t)`) failed symexec at `w->prev = v` (line 22) because QCP's write handler uses only builtin strategies and cannot expand `dlistrep_shape` to access `w->prev` for writing.

4. **Retries (4 refinements attempted)**:
   - **dllseg_shape approach**: Same write-handler limitation
   - **PROP approach** (`w->prev == 0 && dlistrep_shape(w->next, w) * ...`): Symexec PASSED, but 5 proof obligations remain Admitted (entail_wit_1, entail_wit_2_1/2/3, return_wit_1). The strategies cannot convert between `data_at` and `undef_data_at` nor fold/unfold `dlistrep_shape` ↔ `data_at` in the proof context.
   - **has_permission on heap field**: Same 5 Admitted
   - **Explicit data_at** (`data_at(&(w->next), ...) * data_at(&(w->prev), 0) * ...`): Same 5 Admitted

**Root cause**: QCP's write handler for heap field assignments cannot expand custom shape predicates (`dlistrep_shape`/`dllseg_shape`) -- it only fires builtin strategies. Any invariant that exposes `w->prev` via explicit `data_at` or PROP field expressions passes symexec but generates proof obligations the strategies cannot discharge.
